SHIPPING (CARIBBEAN CARGO SHIP SAFETY) REGULATIONS
[SRO 64 of 2001.]

[16th November, 2001.]
1\. Citation
These Regulations may be cited as the Shipping (Caribbean Cargo Ship Safety)
Regulations.

2\. Definitions
In these Regulations—
“Act” means the Shipping Act, Chapter 303;
“anniversary date” means the date in each year corresponding to the date of expiry
of the Certificate;
“appointed surveyor” means a surveyor appointed by the Minister to undertake the
surveys required by these Regulations and includes a surveyor from the Maritime
Administration;
“Caribbean Trading Area” means an area bounded by a line from a point on the
east coast of the United States of America in latitude 35°.00’ North; to a point 5°.00’
South, 33°.00’ West; thence to a point 10°.00’ South, 33°.00’ West; thence to a point
on the coast of Brazil in latitude 10°.00’ South; thence northward along the coast of
Continental America to a point in latitude 35°.00’ North on the east coast of the
United States of America;
“Certificate” means a Caribbean Cargo Ship Safety Certificate issued under these
Regulations;
“Code” means the Code of Safety for Caribbean Cargo Ships as endorsed by the
Memorandum of Understanding on Port State Control in the Caribbean Region signed
at Christchurch, Barbados on 9th February, 1996, and published in the Gazette;
“Director” means the Director of Maritime Affairs as defined in section 4 of the
Act;
“Grenadian ship” has the same meaning as in section 2 of the Act;
“gross tonnage” means gross tonnage as determined by any Shipping (Tonnage)
Regulations made under the Act or made under the U.K. Merchant Shipping Acts and
applied to Grenada;
“international voyage” means a voyage from a port in one country to a port in
another country;
“Maritime Administration” means the office of the Director and such other officers
who are appointed to assist him or her in carrying out the functions of the Act;
“Minister” means the Minister responsible for shipping;
“prescribed” means prescribed by regulations made by the Minister;
“Safety equipment” includes life-saving appliances, fire appliances, lights, shapes,
means of making sound signals, pilot ladders and associated equipment, mechanical
pilot hoists, echo-sounder installations, gyro compass installations, direction-finder
installations and radar installations;
“Safety Regulations” includes any Shipping (Life-saving Appliances) Regulations,
Shipping (Fire Protection) Regulations, Shipping (Safety Construction) Regulations,
Shipping (Safety Radio) Regulations, Shipping (Collision Regulations and Distress
Signals) Regulations, Shipping Navigational Equipment) Regulations, and Shipping
(Pilot Ladders and Hoists) Regulations or other safety regulations made under the Act
or made under the U.K. Merchant Shipping Acts and applied to Grenada.
“Shipping Notice” means a Notice described as such issued by the Director and
any document amending such a Notice;
“tanker” means a cargo ship constructed or adapted for the carriage in bulk of
liquid cargoes of a flammable nature.
3\. Application

Subject to regulation 4 these Regulations apply to all sea-going Grenadian ships of
less than 500 gross tonnage (except passenger ships, fishing vessels and pleasure craft) to
which the Code applies.
4\. Exemption
The Minister may grant exemptions from all or any of the provisions of these
Regulations in respect of classes of cases or individual cases on such terms, if any, as the
Minister specifies.
5\. Ships to be surveyed within twelve months
(1) Every ship to which these Regulations apply must be surveyed in accordance with
regulation 6 no later than twelve months after these Regulations come into operation or at
such later time as the Minister may by Order prescribe.
(2) In the absence of an appointed surveyor the Director may request the assistance of
a surveyor from another State to conduct surveys required by these Regulations and
surveys so conducted are deemed to have been conducted by an appointed surveyor.
6\. Survey before issue of a Certificate
(1) The owner of every ship to which these Regulations apply must cause the same to
be surveyed on completion and thereafter at intervals not exceeding five years by an
appointed surveyor.
(2) The survey referred to in subregulation (1) must include–
(a)

a complete inspection of the structure, machinery and equipment (other than
those items surveyed with the lifesaving appliances and installations) to
ensure that the arrangements, materials, scantlings and workmanship of the
structure, boilers and other pressure vessels, their appurtenances, main
auxiliary machinery, including steering gear and associated control systems,
electrical installations and other equipment comply with the requirements of
the Code and applicable Safety Regulations, are in satisfactory condition and
are fit for the service for which the ship is intended and that the required
stability information is provided;

(b)

an inspection of the pump rooms, cargo, bunker, ventilation and piping
systems and associated safety devices in the case of tankers or barges
carrying liquid cargoes of a flammable nature in bulk;

(c)

an inspection of the outside of the ship’s bottom and related items in drydock, to ensure that they are fit for the service for which the ship is intended;

(d)

a complete inspection of the fire safety system and appliances, life-saving
appliances and arrangements except radio installations, the shipborne
navigational equipment, means of embarkation for pilots and other
equipment to which Chapters 2, 3, 4 and 6 of the Code apply, to ensure that
they comply with the requirements of the Code and applicable Safety
Regulations and are in satisfactory condition and fit for the service for which
the ship is intended;

(e)

a complete inspection of the fire control plans, nautical publications, lights,
shapes and means of making sound signals and distress signals, for the
purpose of ensuring that they comply with the requirements of the Code and
applicable Safety Regulations;

(f)

a complete inspection of the radio installations, including those used in lifesaving appliances, to ensure that they comply with the requirements of the
Code and applicable Safety Regulations.

(3) The results of the survey referred to in subregulation (1) must be recorded in the
ship’s Record of Equipment and Ship Information Book, a copy of which must be kept on
board the ship.
(4) An application for the survey of a ship under this Regulation must be made by or
on behalf of the owner of the ship to the Director and be accompanied by—
(a)

such information relating to the ship as the Director requires for the purposes
of the survey; and

(b)

the prescribed fee.

(5) The appointed surveyor must, upon receipt of an application, survey the ship and
must be satisfied that the ship and its equipment—
(a)

comply with the requirements of the Code and Safety Regulations applicable
to the ship, or are exempt from those requirements; and

(b)

are in all respects satisfactory for the service for which the ship is intended,
having regard to the period for which a Certificate in respect of the ship is to
be issued.

(6) The appointed surveyor, if satisfied on the survey of the above matters, must
forward to the Director a Declaration of Survey containing the particulars of the ship and
its equipment sufficient to enable the Director to issue a Certificate in respect of the ship.
7\. Annual surveys
(1) The owner of every ship in respect of which a Certificate has been issued must, so
long as the certificate remains in force, cause the ship to be surveyed at the intervals
specified in subregulation (2), for the purpose of seeing whether the Certificate should
remain in force, and if the ship is not so surveyed, the Director may cancel the Certificate.
(2) An annual survey must be conducted within three months before or after each
anniversary date of the Certificate.
(3) An application for the survey of a ship under this Regulation must be made by or
on behalf of the owner of the ship to the Director and accompanied by—
(a)

such information relating to the ship as the Director requires for the purposes
of the survey; and

(b)

the prescribed fee.

(4) Upon receipt of an application the appointed surveyor must survey the ship in
accordance with Part I of the Schedule and must be satisfied that—
(a)

the parts of the ship and its equipment specified in Part I of the Schedule
remain efficient; and

(b)

no material alterations have been made in the equipment of the ship to which
the certificate relates without the approval of the Director.

(5) On completion of the survey in accordance with subregulation (4) the appointed
surveyor must, if satisfied, endorse the attachment to the Certificate to that effect.
8\. Periodic survey
(1) The owner of every ship in respect of which a Certificate has been issued must, so
long as the certificate remains in force, cause the ship to be periodically surveyed at the
intervals specified in subregulation (2) for the purpose of seeing whether the Certificate
should remain in force, and if the ship is not so surveyed the Director may cancel the
Certificate.

(2) A periodic survey must be conducted within three months before or after the
secondary anniversary date or within three months before or after the third anniversary
date of the Certificate and should take the place of the annual survey.
(3) An application for the survey of a ship under this Regulation must be made by or
on behalf of the owner of the ship to the Director and accompanied by—
(a)

such information relating to the ship as the Director requires for the purposes
of the survey; and

(b)

the prescribed fee.

(4) Upon receipt of an application the appointed surveyor must survey the ship in
accordance with Part II of the Schedule and must be satisfied that—
(a)

the parts of the ship and its equipment specified in Part II of the Schedule
remain efficient; and

(b)

no material alterations have been made in the equipment of the ship to which
the Certificate relates without the approval of the Director.

(5) On completion of the survey in accordance with subregulation (3), the appointed
surveyor must, if satisfied, endorse the attachment to the Certificate to that effect.
9\. Surveys by marine surveyors
(1) A survey pursuant to regulation 6, 7 or 8 in a port in Grenada or the waters thereof
must be carried out by a marine surveyor from or approved by the Maritime
Administration.
(2) At least one survey pursuant to regulations 6, 7 and 8 in any period of five years
must be carried out by a marine surveyor from the Maritime Administration.
10\. Responsibilities of owner and master
(1) The owner or master of every ship to which these Regulations apply must ensure
that—
(a)

the condition of the safety equipment is maintained so as to comply with the
relevant Safety Regulations and the Code;

(b)

after any survey required by these Regulations has been completed, no material change is
made in the safety equipment subject to such survey without the approval of the Director.

(2) Whenever an accident occurs to a ship or a defect is discovered either of which
affects the safety of the ship or the efficiency or completeness of its safety equipment—
(a)

the accident or defect must be reported at the earliest opportunity to the
Director or to a proper officer of the Maritime Administration;

(b)

the Director or the Minister may cause investigations to be initiated to
determine whether a survey by an appointed surveyor is necessary and must
in that event require such a survey to be carried out.

(3) If the ship is in a port outside Grenada when an accident occurs or a defect is
discovered—
(a)

the master or owner must make a report immediately to the appropriate
authorities of the country in which the port is situated;

(b)

the Director must ascertain from the appropriate authorities of the country in
which the port is situated that a report has been made.

11\. Procedure to be adopted when safety equipment is deficient

(1) If an appointed surveyor determines that the condition of the ship does not
correspond substantially with the particulars of the Certificate or is such that the ship is
not fit to proceed to sea without danger to the ship or persons on board, the surveyor must
advise the owner or master of the corrective action which in the surveyor’s opinion is
required, and must notify the Director.
(2) If corrective action is not taken within such period, being a reasonable period, as
the appointed surveyor specifies, the surveyor must at the end of that time immediately
notify the Director who may, on receipt of such notification, suspend the validity of the
Certificate issued in relation to the ship.
(3) The Director must give notice to the owner and master of any suspension under
subregulation (2).
(4) The master must thereupon deliver up the certificate issued in relation to the ship
to the appointed surveyor on demand and the owner must on receipt of notice of
suspension deliver up the duplicate certificate to the Director.
(5) If the appointed surveyor is satisfied that corrective action has been taken, the
surveyor must notify the Director who must thereupon, if the validity of the certificate has
been suspended—
(a)

restore the validity of the certificate;

(b)

give notice thereof to the owner;

(c)

return the Certificate to the master; and

(d)

return the duplicate certificate to the owner.

(6) If the ship is not within a Grenadian port and corrective action in accordance with
subregulation (3) has not been taken, the appointed surveyor must in addition
immediately notify the appropriate authorities of the country in which the port is situated.
(7) The Director must, at least quarterly, submit a report to the Minister on all
suspensions and cancellations under this Regulation.
12\. Penalties
If a ship to which these Regulations apply proceeds or attempts to proceed to sea
without the requirements of these Regulations having been complied with, the owner and
master of the ship are each guilty of an offence and liable on summary conviction to a
fine of $10,000 and to imprisonment for six months.
13\. Power to detain
If a ship to which these Regulations apply does not comply with the requirements of
these Regulations, the ship is liable to be detained and sections 348 and 444 of the Act,
which relate to the detention of ships, have effect in relation to the ship, subject to the
modification that for the words “this Act” wherever they appear there shall be substituted
“the Act or any regulations made thereunder”.
Schedule
SHIPPING ACT
Annual and Periodic Surveys

[Regulations 7(4) and 8(4).]

## PART I

Annual Surveys

---

### 1.

General
In general the scope of the annual survey should be as follows—
(a)

it should consist of—
(i) an examination of the ship’s certificates, and
(ii) a visual examination of sufficient extent together with certain tests of
the ship and its equipment to confirm that their condition is being
properly maintained;

(b)

it should also include a visual examination to confirm that no unauthorised
modifications have been made to the ship and its equipment;

(c)

the thoroughness or stringency of the survey should depend upon the
condition of the ship and its equipment.

### 2.

Further examination and testing
If any doubt arises as to the condition of the ship or its equipment, further examination
and testing should be conducted as the surveyor deems necessary.
Conduct of Survey

### 3.

Examination of ship’s Certificates
The examination of the ship’s certificates in general should consist of checking—
(a)

the validity of the Caribbean Cargo Ship Safety Certificate, the Cargo Ship
Safety Radio Certificate as applicable and any Exemption Certificate;

(b)

the validity of the International Load Line Certificate, or the International
Load Line Exemption Certificate; and

(c)

an examination, with testing from the local and remote operating locations,
of all watertight doors in watertight bulkhead penetrations as far as is
practicable;

(d)

an examination of the watertight bulkhead penetrations as far as is
practicable;

(e)

confirming, as far as is practicable, that no significant changes have been
made to the structural fire protection arrangements; and

(f)

an examination of the fire doors including their operation.

### 4.

Survey of the machinery and electrical installation
The survey of the machinery and electrical installation should consist of—
(a)

a general examination of the machinery spaces with particular attention to the
propulsion system auxiliary machinery and the provisions against fire and
explosion hazards, confirming that the emergency escape routes are free of
obstructions;

(b)

an examination of all main and auxiliary steering arrangements including
their associated equipment and control systems including their operation;

(c)

testing all means of communication between the navigating bridge and the
machinery control positions and between the navigating bridge and the
alternative steering position, if applicable;

(d)

an examination of the bilge pumping systems and bilge wells as far as
practicable, including the operation of the pumps, remote valve operating
devices and level alarms, if fitted;

(e)

an external examination of the boilers and other pressure vessels together
with their safety devices, foundations, controls, relieving gear, high pressure
and steam escape piping insulation and gauges;

(f)

a general visual examination and, where feasible, an examination in
operation of the electrical machinery, the emergency sources of power, the
switchgear and other electrical equipment;

(g)

confirming, as far as practicable, the correct operation of all emergency
sources of power including, where applicable, their operation automatically;

(h)

an examination of the record of surveys and inspections for the automatic
and remote control systems in ships with periodically unattended machinery
spaces;

(i)

an examination of the official logbook to establish that the steering gear has
been tested as required by any Shipping (Steering Gear and Automatic Pilot
Testing Procedures) Regulations made under the Act.

### 5.

Survey of the life-saving appliances and other equipment
The survey of the life-saving appliances and other equipment should consist of—
(a)

confirmation that any new equipment has been properly approved before
installation and that no changes have been made such as would affect the
validity of the Certificate;

(b)

an examination of the ship’s Official Logbook to establish that the required
musters, training, emergency procedures and inspections of lifeboat
equipment have been carried out as required by any Shipping (Musters)
Regulations made under the Act together with posting of the appropriate
Muster List as required by the said regulations and the Code;

(c)

an examination of all lifeboats, davits, embarkation arrangements and
launching gear in position, as far as practicable, and wherever practicable
with one of the lifeboats lowered to the water;

(d)

an examination of the lifeboat engines, where permitted by cargo handling
conditions, to establish that they will readily start and operate in the ahead
and astern mode;

(e)

checking that the inflatable life rafts have been serviced during the past
twelve months, or if this has not been practicable, that such interval has not
been exceeded by more than three months;

(f)

checking that the stowage will facilitate proper release and that launching
instructions are posted;

(g)

an examination of the arrangement of inflatable life rafts and, where
provided, the arrangement of davit launched life rafts;

(h)

checking the provision of radio equipment for survival craft;

(i)

checking that lifebuoys are in good condition and that the required number
are fitted with self-igniting lights and self-activating smoke signals and that
all are properly stationed;

(j)

checking that rigid life rafts are in good condition and that stowage will
facilitate rapid launching;

(k)

checking that ship and lifeboat distress signals and line-throwing appliance
rockets are not out of date; and

(l)

testing the emergency lighting and general alarm system.

### 6.

Survey of the fire appliances
The survey of the fire appliances should consist of—

(a)

ascertaining whether or not any fire has occurred on board necessitating the
operation of the fixed fire extinguishing system or the portable fire
extinguishers since the last survey;

(b)

confirmation that fire control plans are properly posted;

(c)

examination as far as possible and testing, where feasible, of the fire and/or
smoke detection system(s);

(d)

an examination of the fire main system and confirmation that each fire pump
including the emergency fire pump can be operated separately so that the
two required jets of water can be produced simultaneously from different
hydrants;

(e)

confirmation that fire hoses, nozzles and applicators are in good working
condition and situated at their respective locations;
an examination of fixed fire-fighting system controls, piping, instructions
and marking; checking for evidence of proper maintenance and servicing,
including date of last system tests;

(f)

(g)

confirmation that all non-portable and portable fire extinguishers are in their
correct position along with checking for evidence of proper maintenance and
servicing and conducting random checks for evidence of discharged
containers;

(h)

confirmation, as far as practicable, that the remote controls for stopping fans
and machinery and for shutting off fuel supplies in machinery spaces are in
working order;

(i)

an examination of the closing arrangements for ventilators, funnel annular
spaces, skylights, doorways and tunnel, where applicable;

(j)

confirming that the fireman’s outfits are complete and in good condition and
that the cylinders of any required self contained breathing apparatus are
charged.

### 7.

Survey of the navigational equipment
The survey of the navigational equipment should consist of—
(a)

checking that navigation lights, shapes, sound signalling equipment and
daylight signalling lamps are in order;

(b)

checking that the compass deviation record book is properly maintained;

(c)

checking that the radar, echo-sounder, gyro-compass and direction finder
installation are in working order;

(d)

checking that pilot ladders, associated equipment and mechanical pilot hoists
are in good condition and operational; and

(e)

checking that the nautical publications and charts are adequate for the ship’s
intended trading programme and that they comply with the requirements of
any Shipping (Carriage of Nautical Publications) Regulations made under
the Act and the Code.
Additional Survey Requirements for Tankers

### 8.

Additional survey of tanker
The survey should consist of—
(a)

an external examination of the piping of the cargo pump room fixed firefighting system; and

(b)

confirming that the deck foam system and deck sprinkler system are in
satisfactory operating condition.

### 9.

Tankers with fitted inert gas systems
For inert gas systems, when fitted, the survey should consist of—
(a)

an external examination of the condition of all piping and components for
signs of corrosion or gas leakage/effluent leakage;

(b)

confirming of the proper operation of both inert gas blowers;

(c)

observing the operation of the scrubber room ventilation system;

(d)

checking as far as practicable the deck water seal for automatic filling and
draining and checking for presence of water carry-over and checking the
condition of the non-return valve;
checking as far as practicable the operation of all remotely operated or
automatically controlled valves and, in particular, the flue gas isolating
valve(s);

(e)

(f)

testing the interlocking feature of soot blowers;

(g)

observing that the gas pressure regulating valve automatically closes when
the inert as blowers are stopped; and

(h)

checking as far as practicable the following alarms and safety devices of the
inert as system using simulated conditions where necessary—
(i) high oxygen content of gas in the inert gas main,
(ii) low gas pressure in the inset gas main,
(iii) low pressure in the supply to the deck water seal,
(iv) high temperature of gas in the inert gas main,
(v) low water pressure to the scrubber,
(vi) accuracy of portable and fixed oxygen measuring equipment by means
of calibration gas.

### 10.

Weather deck
On the weather deck the survey should consist of—
(a)

an examination of cargo tank openings including joints, covers coamings and
screens;

(b)

an examination of the cargo tank pressure/vacuum valves and flame
arresting screens;

(c)

an examination, as far as is practicable, of the flame arresting screens on all
bunker, oily ballast and oily slop tanks and void space vents;

(d)

an examination of the cargo, crude oil washing, bunker, ballast and vent
piping systems, including vent masts and heaters; and

(e)

confirming that all electrical equipment in hazardous zones is in good
condition and has been properly maintained.

### 11.

Cargo pump rooms
For cargo pump rooms the survey should consist of—
(a)

confirming that potential sources of ignition and fire in or near the cargo pump
rooms, e.g. loose equipment, excessive product in bilges, excessive vapour,
combustible material, are eliminated and that the access ladders are in good
condition;

(b)

confirming that all electrical equipment is in good condition and has been
properly maintained;

(c)

an examination of all pump room bulkheads for signs of oil leakage or
fractures and, in particular, the penetration seal arrangements;

(d)

an examination of the condition of all piping systems;

(e)

an examination, as far as is practicable, of the cargo, bilge ballast and
stripping pumps for excessive gland seal leakage and verification of the
proper operation of electrical and mechanical remote operating and
shutdown devices;

(f)
(g)

an inspection of the pump room bilge pumping system;
confirming that the pump room ventilation system is operating correctly and
that the ducting is intact, the dampers are operational and the screens are
clean; and

(h)

verifying that the cargo discharge system pressure gauges and the cargo tank
contents gauges are operational.

## PART II

Periodic Surveys
1\. General
The periodic survey must be such as to ensure that the ship and its equipment has been
maintained in accordance with the Safety Regulations and the Code and is in satisfactory
working order.
2\. Compliance with Caribbean Cargo Ship Certificate
The survey should be sufficiently extensive to ensure that the ship’s degree of
compliance with the Caribbean Cargo Ship Safety Certificate warrants the continued
possession of that certificate and that the ship can continue to be operated with safety.
Conduct of Survey

3\. Survey of the hull
(1) The survey of the hull, machinery, appliances and equipment should include all
the relevant items specified in Part I and the following additional items.
(2) The survey should include—
(a)

an examination of the shell including the bottom and bow plating, keel, stem
stern frame and rudder;

(b)

an examination of the sea connections and overboard discharge valves;

(c)

an examination, as far as is practicable, of the anchoring and mooring
equipment, for which purpose the anchors should be partially lowered and
raised using the windlass;

(d)

an examination of at least 2 cargo tanks internally; and

(e)

checking the rudder bearing clearances.

4\. Survey of the machinery and electrical installation
The survey of the machinery and electrical installation should include—
(a)

an examination of the propeller and shaft seals, as far as is practicable,
checking the propeller shaft clearances;

(b)

checking that the surveys of boilers and other pressure vessels have been
carried out in accordance with the applicable Safety Regulations and the
Code; and

(c)

a general examination of the electrical equipment and cables in hazardous
zones such as cargo pump rooms and areas adjacent to cargo tanks, including

checking of the insulation resistance of the circuits, subject to the following
conditions—
(i) records of insulation resistance tests measured by the crew may be
accepted,
(ii) if the condition of the cables, lights, fixtures or equipment appears
defective in any way, insulation resistance measurements must be
required,
(iii) these measurements should be made only when the ship is gas-free,
(iv) insulation resistance testing of intrinsically safe circuits should not
normally be undertaken.
5\. Survey of piping on the weather deck
(1) If upon examination of the cargo, crude oil washing, bunker, ballast, steam and
vent piping there is any doubt as to the condition of the piping it must be pressure tested,
thickness tested, or both.
(2) Particular attention must be paid to any repairs which have been made by welding.
